# Review Package v1.0 — Round-2 probe: does the 2I → SL(2,ℂ) boost half survive the PCD update?

**Artifact:** Round-2 finding of the exact-emergent-Lorentz root campaign (Patch 2060), CPP series_relativity.
This is a FINDING under review, NOT a registered theorem. No THEO/status move has been made; the panel
verdict decides whether the stated world-probability shift is sound before anything is recorded.

## §0. What CPP is, and what this campaign is trying to do (cold-start context)
Conscious Point Physics (CPP) derives Standard-Model structure from a discrete 600-cell polytope substrate
(120 vertices, symmetry group H₄ of order 14400). Conscious Points execute a Perceive→Compute→Displace
(PCD) cycle once per universal Absolute Moment (a global clock tick of duration the Planck time t_P). The
displacement law partitions a fixed per-Moment budget l_P as
  l_P² = (c·Δτ)² + |d_spatial|²,   |d_spatial| = l_P·β   (β = v/c).
After the A3′ amendment the GP→GP broadcast carries a nine-component field 𝔽 = (Φ, V_i, Q_ij)
[scalar l=0, vector l=1, symmetric-traceless tensor l=2].
The root campaign asks whether the PCD dynamics admit an EXACT continuous SO⁺(3,1) action on 𝔽 — a boosted
self-field being an exact rigidly-translating stationary configuration in its co-moving frame, at a
continuum of velocities and all directions, with no lattice drag and no lattice-Cherenkov radiation. Three
worlds: W1 clean algebraic (discrete-exact) bridge; W2 Lorentz exact only in the block-spin continuum limit
with a Planck-suppressed floor; W3 a genuine obstruction (real preferred frame). Budget 10–50 rounds,
committed world-call at round 15.
Round 2 (this artifact) runs the highest-information probe: the 120 vertices are the binary icosahedral
group 2I (unit icosians) ⊂ unit quaternions; the Lorentz group has the quaternionic presentation
SL(2,ℂ) ≅ Spin⁺(3,1). Does the 2I → SL(2,ℂ) boost half survive the PCD update, or does the fixed
Absolute-Moment τ = l_P obstruct the boost generators "at the first commutator"?

## §1. The claim being reviewed (one paragraph)
The naive World-1 static-geometric quaternion bridge fails. (i) 2I ⊂ SU(2) = maximal compact subgroup of
SL(2,ℂ), so it contains NO boost — the vertices supply only the rotation half. (ii) The boost the PCD
budget split manufactures is a COMPACT Euclidean rotation (generator M, M²=−I, angle α=arcsin β), NOT a
Lorentz boost (generator N, N²=+I, β=tanh η). (iii) Three diagnostics confirm the compact real form
so(4)≠so(3,1): collinear composition is circular (β₃=β₁√(1−β₂²)+β₂√(1−β₁²)), reaches β=1 at finite β=1/√2
and is non-monotone beyond; the boost-pair commutator carries the COMPACT real-form sign. The fixed τ=l_P
entering as a +τ² quadrature term is the obstruction (positive-definite form ⇒ compact isometry group).
SCOPE: this kills ONE specific W1 path (the static quaternion bridge); it does NOT claim Lorentz emergence
fails. The Minkowski (−) signature is argued to live in the CAUSAL structure (ds²=(c·t_P)²−|d_spatial|²,
light-cone bound |d_spatial|≤c·t_P), carried by the retarded A3′ broadcast, not the static budget —
relocating W1/W2 to a causal-broadcast route (Round-3 target).

## §2. The claim chain, step by step (what to scrutinize)
S1 — 2I is compact, contains no boost. Vertices as unit quaternions = 2I ⊂ S³ ≅ SU(2). SL(2,ℂ)=SU(2)·{P},
P=exp(½ η n̂·σ) Hermitian-positive (boosts, eigenvalues e^{±η/2}). SU(2) is the maximal compact; unit
quaternions are unitary ⇒ 2I ∩ {boosts}=∅. Vertices supply the rotation half only; the boost half must come
from the PCD dynamics.
S2 — the PCD budget-split boost is a Euclidean rotation. (c·Δτ,|d_spatial|)/l_P=(√(1−β²),β) on the unit
circle; the rest→motion map preserving the positive-definite l_P²=(c·Δτ)²+|d_spatial|² is a Euclidean
rotation by α=arcsin β, generator antisymmetric M=[[0,−1],[1,0]], M²=−I (compact). A genuine boost has
symmetric N=[[0,1],[1,0]], N²=+I (non-compact, β=tanh η). The sign of the generator square is the entire
compact/non-compact (so(4) vs so(3,1)) distinction.
S3 — three falsifiable diagnostics (verify §7).
 (a) collinear composition: budget-split gives circular β₃=β₁√(1−β₂²)+β₂√(1−β₁²); Lorentz gives
     (β₁+β₂)/(1+β₁β₂); diverge at O(β³).
 (b) hard kill: two budget-split boosts at β=1/√2 compose to exactly β=1 (reach c at finite β),
     non-monotone beyond — impossible for a boost group; Lorentz never reaches c.
 (c) real-form sign: the boost-pair commutator lands on the spatial-rotation block with the compact sign
     ([K_i,K_j]=+J for so(4) vs −J for so(3,1)).
S4 — the obstruction is the fixed τ=l_P, internally corroborated. τ enters as +τ² (R_4D²=r_3D²+τ², a 4D
insphere Euclidean relation) ⇒ positive-definite form ⇒ compact SO(4). SR-1's own Appendix H.1 elimination
theorem (no purely geometric displacement model recovers v²/c² scaling) and its energy-momentum bridge
(Lorentz factor injected by hand via ΔSSV=(γ−1)mc²) are exactly what this predicts.
S5 — relocation (the live route). The Minkowski (−) sign is latent: ds²=(c·t_P)²−|d_spatial|² with the
fixed global tick as cone slope and the light-cone bound |d_spatial|≤c·t_P gives the correct proper time
(c·t_P/γ). This signature is causal (the cone), carried by the retarded A3′ GP→GP broadcast (perceive=read
the backward cone), not the static budget partition. The genuine boost generator, if it exists, is N built
from the broadcast kernel, not M from the budget angle. Round 3 tests exactly this.

## §3. Triage — the questions most likely to break the claim (press these hardest)
T1 (signature). Is "positive-definite budget form ⇒ compact real form ⇒ M²=−I boost" correct and complete?
   Any legitimate way the STATIC PCD geometry could carry an indefinite (Minkowski) form this probe missed
   (hidden sign in the projection, a physical — not merely formal — analytic continuation)?
T2 (scope/overclaim). Is the conclusion correctly scoped as "kills the static quaternion-bridge boost," NOT
   "Lorentz emergence fails"? Flag any sentence that overreaches. (Documented overclaim failure mode — be
   ruthless.)
T3 (relocation soundness). Is "the − sign lives in the causal/retarded broadcast, not the static geometry"
   sound, or a hopeful hand-wave? Does ds²=(c·t_P)²−|d_spatial|²=(c·t_P/γ)² actually license attributing
   the Minkowski signature to broadcast causality, or is it bookkeeping any Euclidean theory could write?
T4 (composition law). Is the circular composition law the CORRECT consequence of composing two budget-split
   boosts as the PCD rule defines composition? Could a different physically-motivated composition rule on
   the same budget yield the relativistic law without changing the signature?
T5 (corroboration reading). Is SR-1's H.1 elimination theorem + energy-momentum bridge correctly read as
   corroboration (not as already containing the root)? Any misattribution?

## §4. What a verdict-flipping objection looks like
A verdict-flip on T1–T5 = a demonstration that either (a) the static PCD geometry CAN carry an exact
non-compact boost (reviving the static W1 path), or (b) the relocation to causal broadcast is unsound (so
the probe doesn't narrow the worlds), or (c) the scope is overclaimed. Anything short = calibration: note
it for v1.1 but the finding stands.

## §5. Verification tiers (PD-002) — label every claim
INSPECTED — read and judged sound by eye. INDEPENDENTLY RECOMPUTED — you re-derived the algebra/group
theory yourself. SCRIPT-EXECUTED — you actually ran the §7 code and report its output.

## §6. Read YOUR row (reviewer-specific steer)

  Verdict-honesty: is the world-probability language defensible or inflated?

- Gemini (optional breadth pass) — confirmatory/breadth read; if you run §7, show its output

## §7. Verify code (consistency-evidence ONLY — never proof; run it if you can)
```python
import numpy as np
M = np.array([[0.,-1.],[1.,0.]]); N = np.array([[0.,1.],[1.,0.]])
assert np.allclose(M@M, -np.eye(2)) and np.allclose(N@N, +np.eye(2))
print("M^2=-I (Euclidean/compact) ; N^2=+I (Lorentz/non-compact)  [OK]")
eu = lambda b1,b2: b1*np.sqrt(1-b2**2)+b2*np.sqrt(1-b1**2)   # sin(a1+a2)
lo = lambda b1,b2: (b1+b2)/(1+b1*b2)                          # tanh(e1+e2)
eu_m = lambda b1,b2: np.sin(np.arcsin(b1)+np.arcsin(b2))
lo_m = lambda b1,b2: np.tanh(np.arctanh(b1)+np.arctanh(b2))
for b in [(0.3,0.4),(0.6,0.6),(0.8,0.8),(2**-0.5,2**-0.5)]:
    assert abs(eu(*b)-eu_m(*b))<1e-12 and abs(lo(*b)-lo_m(*b))<1e-12
    print(f"  b1=b2={b[0]:.6f}: EUCLID b3={eu(*b):.6f}  LORENTZ b3={lo(*b):.6f}  |d|={abs(eu(*b)-lo(*b)):.4f}")
bs=2**-0.5
print(f"(a) two Euclidean boosts b={bs:.6f} compose to b3={eu(bs,bs):.6f}  (reaches c at FINITE b; Lorentz can't)")
print(f"(b) Euclidean compose(0.9,0.9)={eu(0.9,0.9):.6f} < compose(.707,.707)={eu(bs,bs):.6f} (non-monotone); Lorentz(0.9,0.9)={lo(0.9,0.9):.6f}")
print(f"(c) at b=1e-3: |Euclid-Lorentz|={abs(eu(1e-3,1e-3)-lo(1e-3,1e-3)):.3e} (agree to O(b^3))")
def emb(g,i):
    G=np.zeros((3,3)); idx=[0,i]
    for a in range(2):
        for b in range(2): G[idx[a],idx[b]]=g[a,b]
    return G
comm=lambda A,B: A@B-B@A
print("Euclidean [M01,M02] =\n", comm(emb(M,1),emb(M,2)))
print("Lorentz   [N01,N02] =\n", comm(emb(N,1),emb(N,2)))
print("-> boost-pair commutator: OPPOSITE sign on the (x,y) block = [K,K]=+J (so4) vs -J (so3,1).")
```

Lorentz 0.994; (c) |Δ|≈1e−9; the two commutators are negatives of each other on the (x,y) block.

# Review Package v1.0 — Round-3 probe: does the A3′ retarded broadcast carry the boost?

This is a FINDING under review, NOT a registered theorem. No THEO / status move made; the panel verdict
decides whether the stated world-call shift is sound before anything is recorded.

## §0. Cold-start context
CPP derives Standard-Model structure from a discrete 600-cell lattice (120 vertices, coordination z=12,
icosahedral point group). Conscious Points execute Perceive→Compute→Displace (PCD) once per universal
Absolute Moment (tick t_P). The root campaign asks whether the PCD dynamics admit an EXACT continuous
SO⁺(3,1) action on the emergent fields, at a continuum of velocities and all directions, with no lattice
drag / no lattice-Cherenkov. Three worlds: W1 exact-discrete (the prize); W2 exact only in the block-spin
continuum limit, Planck-suppressed floor; W3 genuine obstruction (real preferred frame).
Round 2 (already panel-closed SOUND) showed the static PCD budget partition l_P²=(c·Δτ)²+|d_spatial|² is
positive-definite (Euclidean), so its "boost" is a COMPACT rotation (generator M, M²=−I) — the static
quaternion bridge is dead. Panel-endorsed relocation: the Minkowski (−) sign, if any, must come from the
CAUSAL structure (the retarded A3′ broadcast), and ds²=(c·t_P)²−|d_spatial|² is bookkeeping until the
broadcast is shown to dynamically enforce the cone.
Round 3 (this artifact) runs that test: build the boost from the A3′ broadcast kernel and re-run the three
diagnostics.

## §1. The claim being reviewed (one paragraph)
The causal route carries the boost. (A) The A3′ broadcast propagates at a fixed SPEED c (axiom A3:
c=l_P/t_P) on a RETARDED light-cone (C4: h^TT ∝ Q̈(t_ret)), continuum limit the wave operator □ (C3). The
boost preserving a fixed speed (cone slope) is the NON-COMPACT hyperbolic Lorentz boost (generator N,
N²=+I, β=tanh η), giving relativistic addition (β₁+β₂)/(1+β₁β₂) — the inverse of Round 2's compact result.
Conceptual key: the broadcast's invariant is a fixed SPEED, not a fixed LENGTH; the same Absolute Moment
supplies both l_P and c=l_P/t_P, and the boost-relevant invariant is the speed. This RESOLVES the Round-2
T3 caveat: the broadcast dynamically enforces the cone, so the − sign is physical, not bookkeeping. (B)
With the continuum limit Lorentz-invariant, the remaining W1-vs-W2 question collapses to one quantity —
discrete dispersion isotropy (is ω(k)=c|k| exact at finite a, all directions?). 1D at Courant 1 is exact
(single-axis); generic cubic lattices are anisotropic ∝ q² (→ W2); the 600-cell's icosahedral z=12 shell is
anisotropic ∝ q⁴ (~10²–10³× smaller, because icosahedral symmetry has no degree-4 anisotropic invariant).
SCOPE: "carries the boost" is established IN THE CONTINUUM LIMIT (W2 secured, modulo OPEN-SR-9); W1
(exact-discrete) is OPEN and needs the full nested PSR shell-sum to cancel the residual q⁴ exactly. Net
world-call effect (informal, committed call at Round 15): W3 strongly disfavoured, W2 secured floor, W1 the
open upside.

## §2. The claim chain (what to scrutinize)
S1 — the broadcast invariant is a fixed SPEED. Corpus A3: broadcast propagates at c=l_P/t_P. A3′ C3: the
icosahedral PSR shell-sum's continuum limit is □Q=S (wave at exactly c). A3′ C4: retarded far field
h^TT=(2G/c⁴r)Q̈^TT(t_ret). So the broadcast is a finite-speed retarded propagator; its invariant is the cone
slope c, not a budget length.
S2 — fixed-speed ⇒ non-compact boost (Part A). By Einstein's constancy-of-c argument, the frame transforms
preserving a fixed signal speed c are the hyperbolic boosts L(η)=exp(ηN), N²=+I. They fix the null cone
(1,±1) (eigenvalues e^{±η}) and compose by rapidity addition → β₃=(β₁+β₂)/(1+β₁β₂) (<1 always, monotone).
Inverse of Round 2's circle-preserving M (M²=−I, β₃=sin-addition, reached c at finite β). Resolves Round-2
T3: the broadcast's retarded speed-c dynamics enforce the cone — the − sign is dynamical, not an algebraic
rewrite.
S3 — the W1-vs-W2 fork = discrete dispersion isotropy (Part B). Continuum limit is Lorentz-invariant (C3),
so exactness-at-finite-a ⟺ discrete ω(k) exactly linear+isotropic, all n̂. (a) 1D leapfrog at Courant cΔt=a:
ω=ck exactly (verified to 5×10⁻¹⁵) — single-axis exact; the obstruction is all-direction isotropy. (b)
cubic z=6: fractional phase-speed anisotropy ∝ q², no Courant fix → generic W2. (c) icosahedral z=12 (the
600-cell coordination shell): anisotropy ∝ q⁴, ~10²–10³× smaller. Group theory: icosahedral group has no
degree-4 anisotropic invariant (lowest harmonic l=6) vs cubic l=4.
S4 — honest scope. Even icosahedral nearest-neighbour is q⁴-anisotropic, NOT zero → the toy realizes W2
with a tiny floor, not W1. W1 needs the FULL nested PSR shell-sum to cancel the residual exactly — unproven
(Round-4 target). The 600-cell makes W1 uniquely favourable, not automatic.
S5 — world-call effect + forward grounding. W3 (real O(1) preferred frame) strongly disfavoured (the
continuum limit IS Lorentz-invariant; the boost is genuinely hyperbolic). W2 the secured floor (modulo
OPEN-SR-9, the from-substrate c_photon derivation). W1 open with a favourable mechanism. The dispersion-
isotropy decider (Round 4) is identically R2 premise (i)/(ii) (c_photon scalar, not anisotropic f(C,Σ)) and
OPEN-SR-9 — so closing it grounds those corpus items.

## §3. Triage — press these hardest

   speed are boosts") or a disguised assumption ("assume Minkowski, get Minkowski")? Is the input (corpus
   fixed-speed broadcast) genuinely independent of the output (hyperbolic boost)?
T2 (scope / overclaim). Is "carries the boost" correctly confined to the CONTINUUM LIMIT (W2), and W1 kept
   explicitly open? Flag any sentence that lets Round 3 read as establishing exact Lorentz.
T3 (the W1-vs-W2 reduction). Is "exact-discrete ⟺ discrete dispersion isotropy" the CORRECT and COMPLETE
   characterization of W1? Could the discrete broadcast fail (or achieve) exact Lorentz for a reason OTHER

   boost-induced mode mixing among Φ/V/Q)?
T4 (the dispersion numerics + group theory). Is the cubic-q² / icosahedral-q⁴ result correct, and is the
   group-theory claim (no degree-4 icosahedral invariant; lowest harmonic l=6) right? Crucially: is the
   NEAREST-NEIGHBOUR z=12 toy representative of the FULL PSR shell-sum, or could additional shells change
   the scaling (better → toward W1, or worse)?
T5 (corpus reading). Is leaning on C3 ("continuum limit □Q=S") for "W2 secured" legitimate, given C3 is an
   axiom clause (asserted, the from-substrate derivation being OPEN-SR-9)? Is "W2 secured-modulo-OPEN-SR-9"
   the honest statement, or is even W2 less secure than claimed?

## §4. What a verdict-flipping objection looks like
(a) Part A is circular (S2 assumes the answer) ⇒ the causal route does NOT demonstrably carry the boost; or
(b) the W1-vs-W2 reduction is wrong/incomplete (exact Lorentz hinges on something other than dispersion
isotropy) ⇒ Round 3 mislocates the remaining question; or (c) the scope is overclaimed (Round 3 read as
establishing exact Lorentz, or W3 dismissed too strongly). Anything else = calibration.

## §7. Verify code (consistency-evidence ONLY — never proof; run it if you can)
```python
import numpy as np
phi=(1+np.sqrt(5))/2
# PART A — broadcast boost is hyperbolic (cone/speed-c preserving), N^2=+I
def boost(eta): return np.array([[np.cosh(eta),np.sinh(eta)],[np.sinh(eta),np.cosh(eta)]])
N=np.array([[0.,1.],[1.,0.]]); M=np.array([[0.,-1.],[1.,0.]])
assert np.allclose(N@N,np.eye(2)) and np.allclose(M@M,-np.eye(2))
print("broadcast N^2=+I (non-compact) ; Round-2 budget M^2=-I (compact)  [OK]")
for eta in [0.3,0.9,2.0]:
    L=boost(eta)
    assert np.allclose((L@np.array([1,1.]))/ (L@np.array([1,1.]))[0],[1,1.])
    assert np.allclose((L@np.array([1,-1.]))/(L@np.array([1,-1.]))[0],[1,-1.])
print("null cone x=+-ct preserved by every boost (the SPEED c is the invariant)  [OK]")
for b in [(0.3,0.4),(0.6,0.6),(0.8,0.8),(2**-0.5,2**-0.5)]:
    e=np.arctanh(b[0])+np.arctanh(b[1]); L=boost(e)
    assert abs(L[1,0]/L[0,0]-(b[0]+b[1])/(1+b[0]*b[1]))<1e-12
    print(f"  b1=b2={b[0]:.6f}: BROADCAST boost b3={(b[0]+b[1])/(1+b[0]*b[1]):.6f}  (relativistic; <1 always)")
# PART B — discrete dispersion: 1D Courant-1 exact; cubic q^2 vs icosahedral q^4 anisotropy
def w1d(k,S=1.,dt=1.,a=1.): return (2/dt)*np.arcsin(np.clip(S*np.sin(k*a/2),-1,1))
ks=np.linspace(1e-3,np.pi*0.999,400)
print(f"(B1) 1D leapfrog Courant=1: max|w/k-1| over band = {np.max(np.abs(w1d(ks)/ks-1)):.2e}  -> EXACT (single-axis)")
def cub():
    d=[]
    for i in range(3):
        for s in (1,-1):
            v=np.zeros(3); v[i]=s; d.append(v)
    return np.array(d)
def ico():
    pts=[]
    for a_,b_ in [(1,phi),(-1,phi),(1,-phi),(-1,-phi)]: pts+=[(0,a_,b_),(a_,b_,0),(b_,0,a_)]
    P=np.array(pts,float); return P/np.linalg.norm(P[0])
def sym(D,k): return np.sum([2*(1-np.cos(k@d)) for d in D])
def aniso(D,q,nd=400,seed=0):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    g=rng.standard_normal((200,3)); g/=np.linalg.norm(g,axis=1,keepdims=True)
    c2=np.mean([sym(D,1e-4*u)/1e-8 for u in g])
    u=rng.standard_normal((nd,3)); u/=np.linalg.norm(u,axis=1,keepdims=True)
    v=[np.sqrt(sym(D,q*kh)/c2)/q for kh in u]
    return (max(v)-min(v))/np.mean(v)
print("(B2) phase-speed anisotropy (max-min)/mean at fixed q=|k|a:")
for q in [0.2,0.4,0.8,1.2]:
    ac,ai=aniso(cub(),q),aniso(ico(),q); print(f"   q={q:.2f}: CUBIC={ac:.3e}  ICOSA={ai:.3e}  ratio={ac/ai:.1f}")
qs=np.array([0.1,0.15,0.2,0.3,0.4])
sc=np.polyfit(np.log(qs),np.log([aniso(cub(),q) for q in qs]),1)[0]
si=np.polyfit(np.log(qs),np.log([aniso(ico(),q) for q in qs]),1)[0]
print(f"   scaling: CUBIC ~ q^{sc:.1f} ; ICOSAHEDRAL ~ q^{si:.1f}  (icosahedral isotropic to higher order)")
```
Expected: N²=+I/M²=−I OK; null cone preserved; broadcast composition relativistic (β₁=β₂=0.6 → 0.882);
1D Courant-1 |w/k−1| ≈ 5e−15; cubic ~ q² , icosahedral ~ q⁴ , ratio grows ~10²–10³ as q→0.

## Round 4 — the decider, and it lands well

This is the round that resolves the campaign's three-world question down to a single line. The structure of the argument is a no-go that turns out **not to bite CPP**:

**Part A — the periodic no-go (solid, verified).** Any regular *periodic* lattice broadcast has a bounded, Brillouin-zone-periodic dispersion symbol. It can't equal the unbounded ω=c|k| (the phase speed collapses 0.998 → 0.797 as you climb toward the zone edge), and finite shells suppress the icosahedral anisotropy tower (l=6, 10, 15, …) one harmonic at a time — a single z=12 shell gives q⁴; tuning a second φ-shell kills the leading harmonic ×11 but the residual is *nonzero* (the next harmonic). So for a periodic substrate, **exact W1 is impossible** — Lorentz is at best IR-emergent (W2).

**Part B — why it's moot for CPP (the structural turn).** The CPP substrate isn't periodic. SR-1's canonical "Grid Resolution" fixes it as a *"heavily nested array of self-similar 600-cell motifs"* with *"self-similar R/a = φ at every level,"* down to ~l_P/10³⁰. Golden-ratio self-similarity is the defining signature of an **icosahedral quasicrystal** — aperiodic, no Brillouin zone. And independently, a 600-cell admits *no* periodic Euclidean 4-tessellation in the first place (Coxeter: the regular flat-4-space honeycombs are only {4,3,3,4}, {3,3,4,3}, {3,4,3,3}; {3,3,5} tiles the 3-sphere and hyperbolic space, not E⁴). So the substrate is non-periodic **by necessity** — it sits in the no-go-*evading* class by construction. Aperiodicity is exactly the route discrete structures use to carry exact Lorentz (causal sets via randomness; quasicrystals via deterministic self-similarity).

**The determination:** **W3 is excluded** (the IR limit is Lorentz-invariant; any floor is sub-Planck-nesting tiny). The answer is **W1 or W2**, and the W1-vs-W2 line is one sharp, decidable question — *does the φ-self-similar quasicrystal broadcast carry exact Lorentz?* W1 is now **viable specifically because the substrate is golden-ratio self-similar**, where in Round 3 it was merely an "open upside."

**Two honesty notes I want to flag rather than bury:**
1. **W1 is not proven.** Part B is a structural evasion of the no-go plus a corpus reading — not a demonstration that the quasicrystal *is* exactly Lorentz. The finite-shell numerics deliberately don't carry that weight (they can't probe the aperiodic limit; I said so in the script). The real decider — the dispersion/structure-factor isotropy of an explicit icosahedral-quasicrystal approximant — is **Round 5**.
2. **This finding rests on a foundational corpus claim** — that the substrate is quasicrystalline, not periodic — which also surfaces a reconciliation item: the orientation doc's looser "tessellated … per unit cell" language vs SR-1's self-similar-nested reading (they're consistent only under the aperiodic interpretation). §7. VERIFY CODE
import numpy as np
phi=(1+np.sqrt(5))/2
def icosa(r=1.0):
    pts=[]
    for a_,b_ in [(1,phi),(-1,phi),(1,-phi),(-1,-phi)]: pts+=[(0,a_,b_),(a_,b_,0),(b_,0,a_)]
    P=np.array(pts,float); return r*P/np.linalg.norm(P[0])
def symbol(shells,k): return sum(w*np.sum([2*(1-np.cos(k@d)) for d in D]) for D,w in shells)
def aniso(shells,q,nd=600,seed=1):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    g=rng.standard_normal((300,3)); g/=np.linalg.norm(g,axis=1,keepdims=True)
    c2=np.mean([symbol(shells,1e-4*u)/1e-8 for u in g])
    if abs(c2)<1e-12: return np.nan
    u=rng.standard_normal((nd,3)); u/=np.linalg.norm(u,axis=1,keepdims=True)
    v=np.array([np.sqrt(max(symbol(shells,q*kh),0)/abs(c2))/q for kh in u]); m=v.mean()
    return (v.max()-v.min())/m if abs(m)>1e-30 else np.nan
sh1=[(icosa(1.0),1.0)]
rng=np.random.default_rng(0); u=rng.standard_normal((400,3)); u/=np.linalg.norm(u,axis=1,keepdims=True)
ref=np.mean([np.sqrt(max(symbol(sh1,1e-3*kh),0))/1e-3 for kh in u])
for q in [0.3,1.0,2.0,3.0]:
    g=np.mean([np.sqrt(max(symbol(sh1,q*kh),0))/q for kh in u])/ref
    print(f"q={q:4.1f}: v_phase={g:.4f}")
qref=0.15; a_single=aniso(sh1,qref); best=(1e9,0)
for w2 in np.linspace(-1.5,1.5,301):
    a=aniso([(icosa(1.0),1.0),(icosa(phi),w2)],qref)
    if not np.isnan(a) and a<best[0]: best=(a,w2)
print(f"single={a_single:.3e}; best2shell={best[0]:.3e}; x{a_single/max(best[0],1e-30):.0f} but NONZERO")

## All three recommendations done — and Round 5 closes the campaign's central question

**(C) Round 5 — the decider — determines the world is W2.** I went at the last open fork with group theory rather than more shell-tuning, because it's a symmetry fact, not a numerical accident. A structure with **icosahedral point symmetry** — periodic *or* quasicrystalline — is isotropic only up to its first anisotropic invariant, which for the icosahedral group is **degree 6** (there's no l=2 *and no l=4*). Verified to machine precision:

- rank-2 tensor → isotropic (~2×10⁻¹⁶)
- **rank-4 tensor → isotropic** (~3×10⁻¹⁶) — this *is* the isotropic-elasticity theorem; v_phase is isotropic at O(q²)
- **rank-6 tensor → anisotropic** (~0.093) — the l=6 harmonic; v_phase anisotropy enters at O(q⁴), generic and **nonzero**

So the q⁴ anisotropy I measured back in Rounds 3–4 isn't a finite-shell artifact — it's the lowest icosahedral harmonic, and **no structure with merely icosahedral symmetry can remove it.** The quasicrystal inherits it: aperiodicity killed the *periodic* (Brillouin-zone) obstruction, but not the *point-symmetry* one. **Exact W1 would need every anisotropic harmonic to vanish — achieved only by a continuum or a statistically-isotropic *random* (causal-set) substrate** (verified: a Poisson set washes out all harmonics) — and CPP's substrate is a *deterministic* icosahedral quasicrystal, not a random sprinkling. So:

> **The world is W2.** W3: periodic channel excluded (theorem-grade), strongly disfavoured globally — now backed by a *from-substrate* isotropic-elasticity result, not just C3. W1: ruled out for the deterministic 600-cell (modulo one non-generic loophole I flagged honestly — an unmotivated extra symmetry forcing all anisotropic harmonics to vanish). W2: the answer — **IR-exact special relativity from substrate, with Lorentz violation banished to ~10⁻³⁰ of the Planck scale.**

That's the campaign's terminal world-call, reached at **Round 5 of a 10–50 budget**, and it's the handover's "most likely world, itself a strong outcome." Notably it also makes the Round-4 §4.1 further channels (interactions, mode-mixing, C2, composition) **moot** — W1 already fails at the free-dispersion level, the most basic channel, so they never needed separate checking.

import numpy as np
phi=(1+np.sqrt(5))/2
def icosa(r=1.0):
    pts=[]
    for a_,b_ in [(1,phi),(-1,phi),(1,-phi),(-1,-phi)]: pts+=[(0,a_,b_),(a_,b_,0),(b_,0,a_)]
    P=np.array(pts,float); return r*P/np.linalg.norm(P[0])
def qc_approx(levels=4):
    S=[icosa(phi**n) for n in range(levels)]; return np.vstack(S)
def rankk_anisotropy(dirs,k):
    xh=dirs/np.linalg.norm(dirs,axis=1,keepdims=True)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(3); U=rng.standard_normal((400,3)); U/=np.linalg.norm(U,axis=1,keepdims=True)
    f=np.array([np.mean((U[i]@xh.T)**k) for i in range(len(U))])
    return f.std()/abs(f.mean()) if abs(f.mean())>1e-15 else np.nan
def vphase_aniso(dirs,q,nd=500,seed=1):
    D=lambda k: np.sum(2*(1-np.cos(dirs@k)))
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed); g=rng.standard_normal((300,3)); g/=np.linalg.norm(g,axis=1,keepdims=True)
    c2=np.mean([D(1e-4*u)/1e-8 for u in g])
    u=rng.standard_normal((nd,3)); u/=np.linalg.norm(u,axis=1,keepdims=True)
    v=np.array([np.sqrt(max(D(q*kh),0)/abs(c2))/q for kh in u]); m=v.mean()
    return (v.max()-v.min())/m if abs(m)>1e-30 else np.nan
ic=icosa(1.0)
for k in [2,4,6,8]:
    a=rankk_anisotropy(ic,k); print(f"rank-{k}: dev={a:.2e} [{'ISO' if a<1e-9 else 'ANISO'}]")
qc=qc_approx(4); qs=np.array([0.05,0.08,0.12,0.18]); av=[vphase_aniso(qc,q) for q in qs]
print("QC v_phase aniso exponent = q^%.1f" % np.polyfit(np.log(qs),np.log(av),1)[0], "; values", [f"{a:.2e}" for a in av])
rng=np.random.default_rng(7)
for N in [200,2000,20000]:
    P=rng.standard_normal((N,3)); print(f"Poisson N={N}: rank4={rankk_anisotropy(P,4):.2e} rank6={rankk_anisotropy(P,6):.2e}")

§8. RESPONSE FORMAT

SCRIPT OUTPUT (if SCRIPT-EXECUTED):
OTHER NOTES (calibration for v1.1):

**What the result actually says.** The campaign asked a genuinely load-bearing question for CPP: does a discrete substrate reproduce *exact* special relativity, or does the discreteness leave a fingerprint — a preferred frame, a measurable anisotropy? That question matters because the answer could have been near-fatal. If the substrate had forced a real, order-one preferred frame (the "W3" world), CPP would be in immediate tension with one of the most stringently tested symmetries in physics — Lorentz invariance is verified to parts in 10¹⁷ and beyond. So the campaign was, in effect, running CPP into one of the hardest walls modern physics has, to see if it survived. It survived: the answer is W2 — Lorentz invariance exact in the continuum/IR limit, with any violation pushed down to roughly 10⁻³⁰ of the Planck scale, i.e. unobservable by any conceivable experiment. For the framework, that's the result you *need*: CPP is consistent with the body of Lorentz-invariance tests, and it gets there through the substrate's own structure rather than by assumption.

**Where the significance is real, and where I'd temper it.** The genuinely solid core is the group theory in Round 5 — that a structure with only icosahedral point symmetry is isotropic through rank-4 (so its long-wavelength elasticity is exactly isotropic) but carries an unavoidable l=6 anisotropy at the next order. That's standard A₅ representation theory, it checked to machine precision, and three independent engines reproduced it. The honest framing, though, is that the campaign's strongest pieces are *correct applications of already-known results* rather than new physics: that regular lattices only get Lorentz invariance in the continuum limit is textbook lattice field theory; that exact/statistical Lorentz on a discrete set wants randomness is the known lesson of causal-set theory; that icosahedral quasicrystals have isotropic elasticity is established in the quasicrystal literature. So I'd characterize what we did as a careful *placement and consistency* exercise — locating CPP's substrate in a recognized structure class (icosahedral quasicrystal, the H₄/E₈ family) and showing the known theorems land it in the survivable world — not as a discovery. That's worth doing and it was done well, but it isn't a novel prediction.

**The caveats I'd want you to keep in view.** Three of them. First, the whole conclusion is conditional on CPP's own axioms — the A3′ broadcast, the C3 continuum-limit clause, and the reading of the substrate as φ-self-similar/aperiodic. "W2" means "*if* the substrate is as the corpus specifies, *then* W2," not a frame-independent fact about nature. Second — and this is the one I'd flag hardest — the load-bearing inference in Round 5 (that the actual broadcast dispersion inherits the substrate's icosahedral symmetry) is reasonable but it's an *inference*, and the verify scripts model the broadcast as a simple hopping sum, a proxy. The full PCD dynamics with the Φ/V/Q multiplet and the real kernel were never computed; the panel touched this lightly under T2, but a genuinely from-dynamics derivation of the dispersion is still owed. Third, because the residual sits at ~10⁻³⁰ Planck, the result is unobservable — so it's a *consistency* win, not a *discriminating* test. It tells you CPP doesn't contradict relativity; it doesn't give you a measurement that would distinguish CPP from standard physics. And it still leans on C3 and the open OPEN-SR-9 (the from-substrate c_photon), so "exact Lorentz purely from substrate" isn't fully closed.

**How the campaign looks as a piece of work.** Quite good, on its own terms. It reached a terminal, panel-endorsed determination at Round 5 of a 10–50 budget — efficient, well under the Round-15 checkpoint. A process that reliably reins in its own strongest claims is doing the thing review is for.

## Does the DM postulate (qDP/hTetra) need SR-9? No.

What validates your postulate is the **DM-1 arc** (the five-step kill-gauntlet on qDP/hTetra), not DM-2 specifically. DM-2 is just one sub-problem inside it — the structure-formation step. Here's where each step actually stands, and what it depends on:

- **Step 2 (abundance / sea-gravitation gate):** no kill. Its one real requirement — the *uniform* Sea must stay cosmologically inert while its swirl-inhomogeneities gravitate — is gated on **OPEN-SR-5** (the cosmological-constant mechanism), **not** SR-9.
- **Step 3 (coldness):** passes by a wide margin, resting only on the GeV constituent mass. No SR-9.
- **Step 4 (power spectrum) = OPEN-COSMO-DM-2:** **substantially resolved** (Patch 2004) — the EU-1 VSL arc supplies the seeds (n_s = 0.9649), so CPP reproduces P(k). Its residual ledger R1–R4 has only one item touching SR-9: **R2 (the μ↔ε falsifier), and it's at conditional-PASS, explicitly "not a live framework threat."**
- **Step 5 (rotation curve):** passes, riding on c05 *local* gravity. No SR-9.

So the honest read: **you can write the qDP/hTetra identification paper now without SR-9.** SR-9 touches the DM story in exactly one place — the falsifier-clean version of structure formation — and even there it's a conditional-PASS residual, not a blocker. The genuine open dependency for the *complete* DM picture is **OPEN-SR-5** (the Step-2 sea-gravitation gate), which is a different problem entirely.

**Attack strategy.** Write **DM-1 as an identification paper at the established level**: the four passing gates (cross-section, coldness, rotation curve, abundance-no-kill) as the positive case, with the two open dependencies flagged honestly in their own section — sea-gravitation → OPEN-SR-5, falsifier-clean structure formation → R2/OPEN-SR-9. Be candid in it about what the arc itself already flags: the rotation curve is *generic* to any collisionless halo, so it's a consistency gate, not a discriminating win; CPP's actual content is the derived G, the no-new-sector halo (qDP/hTetra are already in the spectrum), and cross-gate consistency. That's a real, publishable identification claim. The discriminating follow-up — deriving ρ(r) from swirl dynamics to predict Tully-Fisher / core sizes — is the thing that would make it *distinctive*, and that's a separate piece of work, not a blocker on the identification paper.

## Can the R2-corollary papers (time dilation, MM, arrow of time) be written now? Mostly yes — and W2 just helped.

This is where the campaign you just closed pays off. Per the root campaign's own corollary map (the 2058 opener §1), those papers are corollaries of the **Lorentz root**, now W2 — and W2 *secures the isotropy leg they were waiting on*. So the dependency splits cleanly:

Writable now at the conditional/effective level.
- **Arrow of time:** the reversibility ladder is a corollary of the root; W2 gives it its kinematic foundation. Writable at the corollary level.

The one thing that genuinely still needs SR-9 is **R2's geometric-Z₀ leg** — i.e., the **μ↔ε / α-stability falsifier paper itself**, which is R2 *proper*. Its unconditional PASS reduces to "is Z₀ geometric?", and that's exactly the SR-9 EM-emergence question, where the 2011 lattice-action attempt came back negative (a DP acoustic mode is a phonon, not the photon).
